Arguments for Statement
3
: The discussion here is similar to that in the proof for Statement
2
above. So, we only highlight the major differences.

Since
\Cr{a:r0Asm},\Cr{a:r1Asm},\Cr{a:ComC}(k),
and
\Cr{a:r2LAsm}
hold, we have
0\leq\rho_{2}<\rho_{1}<1;
also,
1+(2b_{k}-1)\rho_{2}>2b_{k}\rho_{1}.
Further, we have from Lemma
3.7
.
3
that
\varphi_{k}>a_{k},
where
\varphi_{k}
is as in (
3.12
). Combining the two, it is easy to see that
\varphi_{k}\in(0,\infty).

Let
\delta^{L}>0
be as in Lemma
5.8
.
2
and let
\delta\in(0,1)
such that
\delta\leq\min\{\delta^{L},\frac{1}{2\varphi_{k}}\}.
Now, from (
3.8
), it is easy to see that one can write
\mathbb{E}[L_{n,k}^{2}(u_{n})]/\lambda_{n,k}^{2}(u_{n})
as
T_{1}(n)+T_{2}(n)+T_{3}(n),
where

T_{1}(n)=\frac{1}{\lambda_{n,k}^{2}(u_{n})}\sum_{(\bar{t},\,G)\in\Gamma_{n}\times\mathcal{P}_{k}}\mathbb{P}\{\bar{X}_{\bar{t},G}\geq u_{n}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{|V|}\},

T_{2}(n)=\frac{1}{\lambda_{n,k}^{2}(u_{n})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q:\rho_{q}>\delta,\\[1.50694pt]
q\leq Q_{n}\end{subarray}}\;\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\bar{t},\,G),(\bar{t}^{\prime},\,G^{\prime})\in\Gamma_{n}\times\mathcal{P}_{k}:\\[1.50694pt]
(\bar{t},\,G)\neq(\bar{t}^{\prime},\,G^{\prime}),\,\tilde{\rho}^{L}_{(\bar{t},\,G),(\bar{t}^{\prime},\,G^{\prime})}=\rho_{q}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}\{(\bar{X}_{\bar{t},G},\bar{X}_{\bar{t}^{\prime},G^{\prime}})\geq u_{n}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{|V|+|V^{\prime}|}\},

and

T_{3}(n)=\frac{1}{\lambda_{n,k}^{2}(u_{n})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q:\rho_{q}\leq\delta,\\[1.50694pt]
q\leq Q_{n}\end{subarray}}\;\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\bar{t},\,G),(\bar{t}^{\prime},\,G^{\prime})\in\Gamma_{n}\times\mathcal{P}_{k}:\\[1.50694pt]
(\bar{t},\,G)\neq(\bar{t}^{\prime},\,G^{\prime}),\,\tilde{\rho}^{L}_{(\bar{t},\,G),(\bar{t}^{\prime},\,G^{\prime})}=\rho_{q}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}\{(\bar{X}_{\bar{t},G},\bar{X}_{\bar{t}^{\prime},G^{\prime}})\geq u_{n}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{|V|+|V^{\prime}|}\}.

Above,
V
and
V^{\prime}
are the vertex sets of
G
and
G^{\prime},
respectively. Since each
G\in\mathcal{P}_{k}
is a subset of bounded graph
\mathcal{G}_{k}
(see (
3.1
), (
3.2
), and (
3.3
)), it follows, as in our discussion for Statement
2
above, that the upper bound for
q
satisfies
Q_{n}=O(2n+1).

By making use of Lemma
5.5
.
6
, we then have

T_{1}(n)=O\left(\frac{u_{n}^{-1}\exp[-(\varphi_{k}-a_{k})u_{n}^{2}]}{\lambda_{n,k}(u_{n})}\right).

Similarly,

T_{2}(n)=O\left(\frac{u_{n}^{-1}\exp[-(\varphi_{k}-a_{k})u_{n}^{2}]}{\lambda_{n,k}(u_{n})}\right).

Lastly, by using (
5.26
), then adding and subtracting
\left[\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}^{{}^{L}}_{\bar{0},k}(u_{n})]\right]^{2}
and, finally, using Lemmas
5.8
.
2
and
5.5
.
6
, we get

T_{3}(n)=O\left(\frac{u_{n}^{-2b_{k}}\exp[-(\varphi_{k}-a_{k})u_{n}^{2}]}{\lambda_{n,k}(u_{n})}\right)+O\left(u_{n}^{-2}\exp[-2(\varphi_{k}-a_{k})u_{n}^{2}]\right).

Since
2b_{k}\geq 1
for
0\leq k<d,
the desired result is now easy to see.

•

Arguments for Statement
4
: Our discussion here mimics the proofs of Statements
2
and
3
above. So, our arguments are brief. The
k=0
case is trivial. So, suppose that
1\leq k<d.

Set
\delta=1.
From (
3.9
), observe that
\mathbb{E}[D_{n,k}^{2}(u_{n})]/\lambda_{n,k}^{2}(u_{n})
can be written as
T_{1}(n)+\cdots+T_{3}(n),
where

T_{1}(n):=\frac{1}{\lambda_{n,k}^{2}(u_{n})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\bar{t},\,\bar{\Omega})\in\Gamma_{n}\times\tbinom{\langle d\rangle}{b_{k}}:\\[1.50694pt]
\|\bar{t}\|_{\infty}=n\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}\{\bar{X}_{\bar{t},\bar{\Omega}}\geq u_{n}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2b_{k}}\},

T_{2}(n):=\frac{1}{\lambda_{n,k}^{2}(u_{n})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\bar{t},\,\bar{\Omega}),(\bar{t}^{\prime},\bar{\Omega}^{\prime})\in\Gamma_{n}\times\tbinom{\langle d\rangle}{b_{k}}:(\bar{t},\,G)\neq(\bar{t}^{\prime},\,G^{\prime})\\[1.50694pt]
\|\bar{t}\|_{\infty}=\|\bar{t}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}=n,\,\tilde{\rho}_{(\bar{t},\,\bar{\Omega}),(\bar{t}^{\prime},\bar{\Omega}^{\prime})}=\delta\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}\{(\bar{X}_{\bar{t},\bar{\Omega}},\bar{X}_{\bar{t}^{\prime},\bar{\Omega}^{\prime}})\geq u_{n}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}\},

T_{3}(n)=\frac{1}{\lambda_{n,k}^{2}(u_{n})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q:\rho_{q}<\delta,\\[1.50694pt]
q\leq 2dn+2\end{subarray}}\;\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\bar{t},\,\bar{\Omega}),(\bar{t}^{\prime},\bar{\Omega}^{\prime})\in\Gamma_{n}\times\tbinom{\langle d\rangle}{b_{k}}:\\[1.50694pt]
\|\bar{t}\|_{\infty}=\|\bar{t}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}=n,\,\tilde{\rho}_{(\bar{t},\,\bar{\Omega}),(\bar{t}^{\prime},\bar{\Omega}^{\prime})}=\rho_{q}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}\{(\bar{X}_{\bar{t},\bar{\Omega}},\bar{X}_{\bar{t}^{\prime},\bar{\Omega}^{\prime}})\geq u_{n}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}\}.

Using Lemma
5.5
.
2
and the fact that
|\{(\bar{t},\,G)\in\Gamma_{n}\times\tbinom{\langle d\rangle}{b_{k}}:\|\bar{t}\|_{\infty}=n\}|=O((2n+1)^{d-1}),
we have

T_{1}(n)=O\left(\frac{1}{n\lambda_{n,k}(u_{n})}\right),

and

T_{2}(n)=O\left(\frac{1}{n\lambda_{n,k}(u_{n})}\right).

Consider
T_{3}(n).
We first make the following observation. As in (
5.6
), for any
(\bar{t},\,\bar{\Omega})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\tbinom{\langle d\rangle}{b_{k}}
with
\|\bar{t}\|=n,

|\{(\bar{t}^{\prime},\bar{\Omega}^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\tbinom{\langle d\rangle}{b_{k}}:\|\bar{t}^{\prime}\|=n,\tilde{\rho}_{(\bar{t},\,\bar{\Omega}),(\bar{t}^{\prime},\bar{\Omega}^{\prime})}=\rho_{q}\}|\leq{}_{,k}\;q^{d-2}

for some
k-
dependent constant
{\Cr{c:upBdRhon}}_{,k}\geq 0.
Because
\Cr{a:CLTG}
holds, we trivially have that

\sum_{q=0}^{\infty}q^{d-2}\rho_{q}<\infty.

Separately, observe that, for any
(\bar{t},\,\bar{\Omega}),(\bar{t}^{\prime},\bar{\Omega}^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\tbinom{\langle d\rangle}{b_{k}}
with
\tilde{\rho}_{(\bar{t},\,\bar{\Omega}),(\bar{t}^{\prime},\bar{\Omega}^{\prime})}=\rho_{q},

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\{(\bar{X}_{\bar{t},\bar{\Omega}},\bar{X}_{\bar{t}^{\prime},\bar{\Omega}^{\prime}})\geq u_{n}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}\}-\left[\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}_{\bar{0},\langle b_{k}\rangle}]\right]^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\{(\bar{X}_{\bar{t},\bar{\Omega}},\bar{X}_{\bar{t}^{\prime},\bar{\Omega}^{\prime}})\geq u_{n}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}\}-\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}_{\bar{t},\bar{\Omega}}(u_{n})]\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}_{\bar{t}^{\prime},\bar{\Omega}^{\prime}}(u_{n})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O\left(\rho_{q}\;u_{n}^{-(4b_{k}-2)}\exp\left[\frac{-2a_{k}u_{n}^{2}}{1+2a_{k}\rho_{q}}\right]\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O\left(\rho_{q}\;u_{n}^{-(4b_{k}-2)}\exp\left[\frac{-2a_{k}u_{n}^{2}}{1+2a_{k}\rho_{1}}\right]\right)

where the first relation follows since
X
is stationary, the next one holds due to Lemma
5.7
.
4
, while the last holds since
\rho_{q}\leq\rho_{1}
which itself is true due to
\Cr{a:r2LAsm}.
Thus, by adding and subtracting
\left[\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}_{\bar{0},\langle b_{k}\rangle}]\right]^{2}
to the summand in
T_{3}(n),
it then follows, as in the proof of Statement
2
above, that

T_{3}(n)=O\left(\frac{u_{n}^{-(2b_{k}-2)}}{n\lambda_{n,k}(u_{n})}\exp\left[\frac{-a_{k}(1-2a_{k}\rho_{1})u_{n}^{2}}{1+2a_{k}\rho_{1}}\right]\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{n^{2}}\right).

The desired result is now easy to see.

This completes the proof.
∎

Remark 5.9
.

Consider
\mathds{1}^{{}^{N}}_{\bar{t},k}(u)
and
\mathds{1}^{{}^{L}}_{\bar{t},k}(u)
as in (
5.21
) and (
5.22
), respectively. Then, using ideas from the above proof, it is not difficult to see that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\textnormal{Var}[\sum_{\bar{t}\in\Gamma_{n}}\mathds{1}^{{}^{N}}_{\bar{t},k}(u_{n})]}{\lambda_{n,k}(u_{n})}=0

if
1\leq k<d-1
and
\Cr{a:r0Asm},\Cr{a:CLTG},\Cr{a:r2Asm}
and
\Cr{a:rqAsm}
hold; while

\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\textnormal{Var}[\sum_{\bar{t}\in\Gamma_{n}}\mathds{1}^{{}^{L}}_{\bar{t},k}(u_{n})]}{\lambda_{n,k}(u_{n})}=0

if
0\leq k<d
and
\Cr{a:r0Asm},\Cr{a:r1Asm},\Cr{a:ComC}(k),\Cr{a:CLTG},
and
\Cr{a:r2LAsm}
hold.

Finally, we discuss the proof of Theorem
3.10
.

Proof of Theorem
3.10
.

The proof is essentially the same for
k=0
and
1\leq k<d
cases. Therefore, we detail the steps only for the latter.

As in the proof of Theorem
3.8
, (
5.7
) holds. Bounds for the two terms on the RHS there have already been obtained in the proof of Lemma
3.9
.
1
. Therefore, we have

\|S_{n,k}(u_{n})-\textnormal{Poi}(\mathbb{E}[S_{n,k}(u_{n})])\|_{\textnormal{TV}}=O\left(u_{n}^{-1}\exp[-(\varphi_{k}-a_{k})u_{n}^{2}]\right).

(5.32)

Again, since
\Cr{a:r1Asm}
and
\Cr{a:r2LAsm}
imply
\Cr{a:r2Asm}
and because
\nu_{n}\to-\infty
is equivalent to
\lambda_{n,k}(u_{n})\to\infty,
it follows from Lemma
3.7
.
1
that
\mathbb{E}[S_{n,k}(u_{n})]\to\infty.
Therefore, we have

\frac{\textnormal{Poi}(\mathbb{E}[S_{n,k}(u_{n})])-\mathbb{E}[S_{n,k}(u_{n})]}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[S_{n,k}(u_{n})]}}\Rightarrow N(0,1);

this can be easily seen by using the characteristic function. Separately, using (
5.7
) and Definition
2.6
,

\displaystyle\left\|\left[\frac{S_{n,k}(u_{n})-\mathbb{E}[S_{n,k}(u_{n})]}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[S_{n,k}(u_{n})]}}\right]-\left[\frac{\textnormal{Poi}(\mathbb{E}[S_{n,k}(u_{n})])-\mathbb{E}[S_{n,k}(u_{n})]}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[S_{n,k}(u_{n})]}}\right]\right\|_{\textnormal{TV}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|S_{n,k}(u_{n})-\textnormal{Poi}(\mathbb{E}[S_{n,k}(u_{n})])\|_{\textnormal{TV}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O\left(u_{n}^{-1}\exp[-(\varphi_{k}-a_{k})u_{n}^{2}]\right).

Therefore, combining the above two relations, it follows that

\frac{S_{n,k}(u_{n})-\mathbb{E}[S_{n,k}(u_{n})]}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[S_{n,k}(u_{n})]}}\Rightarrow N(0,1).

Now, since Lemma
3.7
.
1
holds, we get the desired result.
∎

Remark 5.10
.

We note that the above result could have been proved using Berman’s idea (
[
10
]
, Chapter 8), where he showed a CLT for the sojourn time of a continuous time Gaussian process. In order to understand the technique, let us focus first on proving a CLT for the number of exceedances
S_{n,0}(u_{n})=\sum_{\bar{t}\in\Gamma_{n}}1(X_{\bar{t}}\geq u_{n})
. If the process
X
is stationary with absolutely continuous spectral distribution function, we have the following representation for its covariance function:

\mathbb{E}[X_{0}X_{\bar{t}}]=\rho(\bar{t})=\sum_{m_{1}=-\infty}^{\infty}\sum_{m_{2}=-\infty}^{\infty}\cdots\sum_{m_{d}=-\infty}^{\infty}b(m_{1},m_{2},\cdots,m_{d})b(t_{1}+m_{1},t_{2}+m_{2},\cdots,t_{d}+m_{d})

for some
b\in l^{2}
(can be chosen as inverse Fourier transform of the spectral density). This also means that we have the following representation for
X
:

X_{\bar{t}}=\sum_{m_{1}=-\infty}^{\infty}\sum_{m_{2}=-\infty}^{\infty}\cdots\sum_{m_{d}=-\infty}^{\infty}b(t_{1}+m_{1},t_{2}+m_{2},\cdots,t_{d}+m_{d})\zeta_{m_{1}\cdots m_{d}}

with
\zeta
being IID Gaussians.

The main idea is to show that the limiting distribution of exceedances of
X
is the same as that of an auxiliary process
X_{v}
for some
v\in\mathbb{R}
. To define this process, first let

b_{v}(\bar{t})=b_{\bar{t}},\,\,\,|t_{i}|\leq\left\lceil\frac{v}{2}\right\rceil\,\,\forall i,

and zero otherwise. Using this, define the auxiliary process by

X_{v}(\bar{t})=\frac{\sum_{m_{1}=-\infty}^{\infty}\sum_{m_{2}=-\infty}^{\infty}\cdots\sum_{m_{d}=-\infty}^{\infty}b(t_{1}+m_{1},t_{2}+m_{2},\cdots,t_{d}+m_{d})\zeta_{m_{1}m_{2}\cdots m_{d}}}{\sqrt{\sum_{m_{1}=-\infty}^{\infty}\sum_{m_{2}=-\infty}^{\infty}\cdots\sum_{m_{d}=-\infty}^{\infty}b_{v}^{2}(m_{1},\cdots,m_{d})}}.

The core concept then is that the distribution of exceedances of this auxiliary process can be boiled down to studying limits of sums of i.i.d. random variables.

It is also worth noting that one can generalize the above to random vectors in order to study the limiting behaviour of
S_{n,k}(u_{n})
for
k\geq 1
.

6
Completing the Proofs

The technical results from Section
5
are proved here.

For an arbitrary
m\geq 1
and
\rho\in\mathbb{R},
let
W_{m}(\rho)
be the
m\times m
matrix whose diagonal entries are all
1
and the off-diagonal entries are all equal to
\rho.
Similarly, for
m_{1},m_{2}\geq 1
and
\rho,\mu\in\mathbb{R},
let
W_{m_{1},m_{2}}(\rho,\mu)
be the
(m_{1}+m_{2})\times(m_{1}+m_{2})
matrix given by

W_{m_{1},m_{2}}(\rho,\mu):=\begin{bmatrix}W_{m_{1}}(\rho)&\mu\times\mathbf{1}_{m_{1},m_{2}}\\
\mu\times\mathbf{1}_{m_{2},m_{1}}&W_{m_{2}}(\rho)\end{bmatrix},

(6.1)

where
\mathbf{1}_{i,j}
denotes the all ones matrix of dimension
i\times j.
Further, for
m\geq 2,

1\leq m_{1},m_{2}<m,
and
\rho,\mu^{\prime},\mu\in\mathbb{R},
let
Q_{m,m_{1},m_{2}}(\rho,\mu^{\prime},\mu)\in\mathbb{R}^{2m\times 2m}
be given by

Q_{m,m_{1},m_{2}}(\rho,\mu^{\prime},\mu)=\begin{bmatrix}W_{m_{1},m-m_{1}}(\rho,\mu^{\prime})&\mu\mathbf{1}_{m,m}\\
\mu\mathbf{1}_{m,m}&W_{m_{2},m-m_{2}}(\rho,\mu^{\prime})\end{bmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{2m\times 2m}.

(6.2)

Separately, for a rational function
h
, let
\textnormal{den}(h)
denote its denominator. Lastly, given vectors
\bar{u}_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{m_{1}},
and
\bar{u_{2}}\in\mathbb{R}^{m_{2}},
let
(\bar{u}_{1},\bar{u}_{2})
denote the row vector of dimension
m_{1}+m_{2}
whose first
m_{1}
entries are those of
\bar{u_{1}}
and the last
m_{2}
entries are those of
\bar{u}_{2}.

We begin by deriving the probability estimates stated in Lemma
5.5
.

Proof of Lemma
5.5
.

The truth of each statement can be seen from Lemma
2.4
and the following lines of argument. Below, the notations
i,

M,
and
\bar{\Delta}
are as in Lemma
2.4
, while
u
is an arbitrary but fixed positive real number.

•

Arguments for Statement
1
: Because
\Cr{a:r0Asm}
holds,
X_{\bar{0}}\sim N(0,1).
With respect to
\mathbb{P}\{X_{\bar{0}}\geq u\},
we thus have
i=1,

M=1,
and
\bar{\Delta}=u.
As
u
is positive, the Savage condition holds trivially. Hence, from Lemma
2.4
, it follows that

1-\frac{1}{u^{2}}\leq\dfrac{\mathbb{P}\{X_{\bar{0}}\geq u\}}{\tau_{0}u^{-2b_{0}}\exp[-a_{0}u^{2}]}\leq 1.

This completes the argument.

•

Arguments for Statement
2
: Since
\Cr{a:r0Asm}
holds,
\bar{X}_{\bar{0},\langle b_{k}\rangle}\sim N(\bar{0},W_{2b_{k}}(\rho_{2})).
On the other hand, because
\Cr{a:r2Asm}
holds, it follows from Lemma
A.1
that
W_{2b_{k}}(\rho_{2})
is positive definite with eigenvalues
(1-\rho_{2}),
repeated
2b_{k}-1
times, and
(1+(2b_{k}-1)\rho_{2});
also,
\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2b_{k}}
is the eigenvector with eigenvalue
1+(2b_{k}-1)\rho_{2}.
With
i=2b_{k},
and
M=W_{2b_{k}}(\rho_{2}),
we then have

|M|=(1-\rho_{2})^{2b_{k}-1}(1+(2b_{k}-1)\rho_{2}),

[u\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2b_{k}}]M^{-1}[u\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2b_{k}}]^{\top}=2a_{k}u^{2},

and

\bar{\Delta}=[u\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2b_{k}}]M^{-1}=[u/(1+(2b_{k}-1)\rho_{2})]\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2k+2}>0.

The latter verifies the Savage condition and also shows that

\prod_{j=1}^{2b_{k}}\bar{\Delta}(j)=\left[\frac{u}{1+(2b_{k}-1)\rho_{2}}\right]^{2b_{k}}\text{ and }\bar{\Delta}(j)\bar{\Delta}(\ell)=\left[\frac{u}{1+(2b_{k}-1)\rho_{2}}\right]^{2}.

From Lemma
2.4
, we then have

1-\left[\dfrac{[1+(2b_{k}-1)\rho_{2}]^{2}\sum_{j,\ell=1}^{i}M_{j\ell}(1+\delta_{j\ell})}{2}\right]\frac{1}{u^{2}}\leq\dfrac{\mathbb{P}\{\bar{X}_{\bar{0},\langle b_{k}\rangle}\geq u\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2b_{k}}\}}{\left[\tau_{k}/\tbinom{d}{b_{k}}\right]u^{-2b_{k}}\exp[-a_{k}u^{2}]}\leq 1,

Because
\sum_{j,\ell=1}^{i}M_{j\ell}(1+\delta_{j\ell})
is a constant, we are done.

•

Arguments for Statement
3
: We use divide and conquer strategy. In that, we first classify the elements of
\mathcal{N}_{k}
based on
|V_{1}|,
where
V_{1}
is the first subset in the partition uniquely associated with each
G\in\mathcal{N}_{k};
see Remark
5.4
and Lemma
4.14
. We then obtain an upper bound for
\mathbb{P}\{\bar{X}_{\bar{t},G}\geq u\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2b_{k}}\}
that holds for every
G
in a class. Thereafter, identifying the relationship between the bounds for each class, we obtain an universal bound.

To begin with, we derive some basic facts with regards to
W_{j,2b_{k}-j}(\rho_{2},\rho_{3})
for
1\leq j\leq b_{k}.
Because
\Cr{a:r0Asm},\Cr{a:r2Asm},
and
\Cr{a:rqAsm}
hold, we have

0\leq\rho_{3}<\rho_{2}<\rho_{0}=1.

(6.3)

This implies that
1+(j-1)\rho_{2}-j\rho_{3}
and
1+(2b_{k}-1-j)\rho_{2}-(2b_{k}-j)\rho_{3}
are positive. From Lemma
A.3
, it then follows that
W_{j,2b_{k}-j}(\rho_{2},\rho_{3})
is positive definite. Further, from its definition, we also have that this matrix is symmetric. Both these facts, put together, show that
Y_{j}\sim N(\bar{0},W_{j,2b_{k}-j}(\rho_{2},\rho_{3}))
is well defined.

Separately, for
1\leq j\leq 2b_{k}-1,
let

r_{j}=\frac{1-\rho_{3}+(2b_{k}-1-j)(\rho_{2}-\rho_{3})}{1-\rho_{3}^{2}+(2b_{k}-2)(\rho_{2}-\rho_{3}^{2})+(j-1)(2b_{k}-1-j)(\rho_{2}^{2}-\rho_{3}^{2})},

and

\varrho_{k,j}:=jr_{j}+(2b_{k}-j)r_{2b_{k}-j}.

Then, using brute force, it is not difficult to see that

\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2b_{k}}\;W^{-1}_{j,2b_{k}-j}(\rho_{2},\rho_{3})=(r_{j}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{j},r_{2b_{k}-j}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2b_{k}-j})

and

\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2b_{k}}\;[W_{j,2b_{k}-j}(\rho_{2},\rho_{3})]^{-1}\;\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2b_{k}}^{\top}=\varrho_{k,j}

for
1\leq j\leq b_{k}.
Clearly,
\textnormal{den}(r_{j})=\textnormal{den}(r_{2b_{k}-j});
this implies that
\textnormal{den}(\varrho_{k,j})=\textnormal{den}(r_{j}).
Further, since (
6.3
) holds, both the numerator and denominator of each
r_{j}
are positive. This implies that, for each
1\leq j\leq b_{k},

\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2b_{k}}\;W^{-1}_{j,2b_{k}-j}(\rho_{2},\rho_{3})>0\text{ and }\varrho_{k,j}>0.

(6.4)

Lastly, for
1\leq j\leq b_{k}-1,
by using brute force or a symbolic calculator, we have

\varrho_{k,j+1}-\varrho_{k,j}=\dfrac{2(2(b_{k}-1)-2j+1)(\rho_{2}-\rho_{3})(1-\rho_{2})(1-\rho_{3}+(b_{k}-1)(\rho_{2}-\rho_{3}))}{\textnormal{den}(\varrho_{k,j})\textnormal{den}(\varrho_{k,j+1})};

hence, and since (
6.3
) holds, we get
\varrho_{k,j+1}-\varrho_{k,j}>0.
In other words,

\varrho_{k,1}=\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2b_{k}}[W^{-1}_{1,2b_{k}-1}(\rho_{2},\rho_{3})]\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2b_{k}}^{\top}<\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2b_{k}}[W^{-1}_{j,2b_{k}-j}(\rho_{2},\rho_{3})]\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2b_{k}}^{\top}=\varrho_{k,j}

(6.5)

for each
j
such that
2\leq j\leq b_{k}.

Now, fix some arbitrary
G\in\mathcal{N}_{k}
and suppose that
|V_{1}|=m.
Lemma
4.14
implies that
\|\bar{t}-\bar{t}^{\prime}\|_{1}\geq 3
for
\bar{t}\in V_{1}
and
\bar{t}^{\prime}\in V\setminus V_{1}.
Further, if
\bar{t},\bar{t}^{\prime}
are both in
V_{1}
or are both in
V_{2},
then it follows from Corollary
4.8
that
\|\bar{t}-\bar{t}^{\prime}\|_{1}\geq 2.
Since
\Cr{a:r0Asm}
and
\Cr{a:rqAsm}
hold, after a permutation of the coordinates of
\bar{X}_{\bar{0},G}
if necessary, it now follows from Lemma
2.5
that

\mathbb{P}\{\bar{X}_{\bar{0},G}\geq u\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2b_{k}}\}\leq\mathbb{P}\{\bar{Y}_{m}\geq u\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2b_{k}}\}.

Consider
\mathbb{P}\{\bar{Y}_{m}\geq u\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2b_{k}}\}.
With
i=2b_{k}
and
M=W_{m,2b_{k}-m}(\rho_{2},\rho_{3}),
(
6.4
) shows that

\bar{\Delta}:=[u\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2b_{k}}]\;M^{-1}>0,

(6.6)

which verifies the Savage condition. From Lemma
2.4
, we then have

\mathbb{P}\{\bar{Y}_{m}\geq u\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2b_{k}}\}\leq\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{b_{k}}|M|^{1/2}\prod_{l=1}^{2b_{k}}\bar{\Delta}(l)}\;\exp\left[-\frac{\varrho_{k,m}u^{2}}{2}\right].

(6.7)

Combining this with (
6.5
), we finally have

\mathbb{P}\{\bar{Y}_{m}\geq u\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2b_{k}}\}\leq{}_{,k}\;u^{-2b_{k}}\exp\left[-\frac{\varrho_{k,1}u^{2}}{2}\right],

(6.8)

where

{\Cr{c:PrNnk}}_{,k}:=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{b_{k}}}\max_{1\leq j\leq b_{k}}\left[\dfrac{1}{|W_{j,2b_{k}-j}(\rho_{2},\rho_{3})|^{1/2}\prod_{l=1}^{2b_{k}}\bar{\Delta}^{\prime}_{j}(l)}\right]

with
\bar{\Delta}^{\prime}_{j}=\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2b_{k}}W^{-1}_{j,2b_{k}-j}(\rho_{2},\rho_{3}).
Note that
\varrho_{k,1}/2=\varrho_{k},
where the latter is as in (
3.11
). Since
G
was arbitrary, this line of argument is now complete.

•

Arguments for Statement
4
: Consider
\mathbb{P}\{(X_{\bar{0}},X_{\bar{e}_{1}})\geq u\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2}\}.
With regards to this expression,
i=2
and
M=W_{2}(\rho_{1}),
where the latter is on account of
\Cr{a:r0Asm}.
Clearly,

|M|=(1-\rho_{1})(1+\rho_{1}),

[u\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2}]M^{-1}[u\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2}]^{\top}=2u^{2}/(1+\rho_{1}),

and

\bar{\Delta}=[u/(1+\rho_{1})]\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2}>0.

The latter verifies the Savage condition and also shows that
\prod_{j=1}^{2}\bar{\Delta}(j)=\frac{u^{2}}{(1+\rho_{1})^{2}}.
Hence, from Lemma
2.4
, we have

\mathbb{P}\{(X_{\bar{0}},X_{\bar{e}_{1}})\geq u\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2}\}\leq\frac{(1+\rho_{1})^{2}}{2\pi\sqrt{1-\rho_{1}^{2}}}u^{-(2b_{0}+1)}\exp[-\varphi_{0}u^{2}],

where
\varphi_{0}
is as in (
3.12
). We are now done.

•

Arguments for Statement
5
: Here,
i=2b_{k}+1
and
M=W_{2b_{k},1}(\rho_{2},\rho_{1}).
As
\Cr{a:r1Asm}
and
\Cr{a:ComC}(k)
hold, it follows from Lemma
A.3
that
M
is positive definite. Now, by brute force,

[u\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2b_{k}+1}]M^{-1}[u\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2b_{k}+1}]^{\top}=u^{2}\,[(2b_{k})r_{2b_{k}}+r_{1}]=2u^{2}\varphi_{k}

and

\bar{\Delta}=[u\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2b_{k}+1}]M^{-1}=(r_{2b_{k}}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2b_{k}},\;r_{1})\,u,

where
\varphi_{k}
is as in (
3.12
) and

r_{j}=\frac{1+(2b_{k}-j)\rho_{2}-(2b_{k}+1-j)\rho_{1}}{[1+(2b_{k}-1)\rho_{2}][1-\rho_{1}]+\rho_{1}[1+(2b_{k}-1)\rho_{2}-(2b_{k})\rho_{1}]}

for
j=1,2b_{k}.
Since
\Cr{a:r1Asm}
and
\Cr{a:ComC}(k)
hold, both the numerator and denominator of
r_{1}
and
r_{2b_{k}}
are positive. This shows that
\bar{\Delta}>0,
which verifies the Savage condition. Hence, from Lemma
2.4
,

\mathbb{P}\{(\bar{X}_{\bar{0},\langle b_{k}\rangle},X_{\bar{0}})\geq u\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2b_{k}+1}\}\leq\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{(2b_{k}+1)/2}|M|^{1/2}\prod_{j=1}^{2b_{k}+1}\bar{\Delta}^{\prime}(j)}u^{-(2b_{k}+1)}\exp[-\varphi_{k}u^{2}].

With this, we are done.

•

Arguments for Statement
6
: Let
k\geq 1.
Fix an arbitrary
G\equiv(V,E)\in\mathcal{P}_{k}.
From (
3.1
) and (
3.2
),
|V|\geq 2b_{k}+1;
also, there is further subset of
2b_{k}
vertices whose pairwise
\|\cdot\|_{1}-
distances are at least
2.
Retaining these latter
2b_{k}
vertices and choosing any one of the remaining vertices, it is easy to see using Lemma
2.5
and
\Cr{a:r2LAsm}
that

\mathbb{P}\{\bar{X}_{\bar{0},G}\geq u\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{|V|}\}\leq\mathbb{P}\{(\bar{X}_{\bar{0},\langle b_{k}\rangle},X_{\bar{0}})\geq u\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2b_{k}+1}\}.

The desired result now follows from Statement
5
, proved above.

Now consider the case
k=0.
Let
G\equiv(V,E)\in\mathcal{P}_{0}.
From (
3.3
), note that
|V|=2.
Because
\Cr{a:r2LAsm}
holds, it follows from Lemma
2.5
that

\mathbb{P}\{\bar{X}_{\bar{0},G}\geq u\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2}\}\leq\mathbb{P}\{(X_{\bar{0}},X_{\bar{e}_{1}})\geq u\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2}\}.

The desired result now follows from Statement
4
, proved above.

This completes the proof.
∎

We next derive the covariance bounds discussed in Lemma
5.7
.

Proof of Lemma
5.7
.

We handle each statement separately. In each case, the idea is to first approximate
\bar{X}_{(\bar{t},\,\bar{\Omega}),(\bar{t}^{\prime},\bar{\Omega}^{\prime})}
by a simpler Gaussian vector. In that regard, we first introduce a notation. For
k
such that
1\leq k<d
and
\mu\in[0,\rho_{1}],
let
\bar{Z}_{\mu}\sim N(\bar{0},W_{2b_{k},2b_{k}}(\rho_{2},\mu)),
where
W_{2b_{k},2b_{k}}(\rho_{2},\mu)
is as in (
6.1
). Because
\mu\in[0,\rho_{1}]
and
\Cr{a:ComC}(k)
holds, we have
1+(2b_{k}-1)\rho_{2}-2b_{k}\mu\geq 1+(2b_{k}-1)\rho_{2}-2b_{k}\rho_{1}>0.
Using Lemma
A.3
, it then follows that the matrix
W_{2b_{k},2b_{k}}(\rho_{2},\mu)
is positive definite; by definition, it is also symmetric. Consequently,
\bar{Z}_{\mu}
is well defined for each
\mu\in[0,\rho_{1}].

•

Arguments for Statement
1
: If
(\bar{t},\,\bar{\Omega})=(\bar{t}^{\prime},\bar{\Omega}^{\prime}),
then the non-negativity trivially follows as the covariance then is simply the variance. Suppose
(\bar{t},\,\bar{\Omega})\neq(\bar{t}^{\prime},\bar{\Omega}^{\prime}).
Now, let

\hat{\rho}_{(\bar{t},\,\bar{\Omega}),(\bar{t}^{\prime},\bar{\Omega}^{\prime})}:=\min\{\rho_{\|(\bar{t}+\alpha\bar{e}_{\omega})-(\bar{t}^{\prime}+\alpha^{\prime}\bar{e}_{\omega^{\prime}})\|_{1}}:\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\in\{-1,+1\},\;\omega\in\bar{\Omega},\omega^{\prime}\in\bar{\Omega}^{\prime}\}.

As
(\bar{t},\,\bar{\Omega})\neq(\bar{t}^{\prime},\bar{\Omega}^{\prime}),
we have
\hat{\rho}_{(\bar{t},\,\bar{\Omega}),(\bar{t}^{\prime},\bar{\Omega}^{\prime})}\neq\rho_{0};
in fact, due to
\Cr{a:r1Asm}
and
\Cr{a:r2LAsm},

\hat{\rho}_{(\bar{t},\,\bar{\Omega}),(\bar{t}^{\prime},\bar{\Omega}^{\prime})}\in[0,\rho_{1}].

Hence, the random vector
\bar{Z}_{\hat{\rho}_{(\bar{t},\,\bar{\Omega}),(\bar{t}^{\prime},\bar{\Omega}^{\prime})}}
is well defined. Separately, note that

\mathbb{P}\{\bar{Z}_{0}\geq u_{n}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}\}=\mathbb{P}\{\bar{X}_{\bar{t},\bar{\Omega}}\geq u_{n}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2b_{k}}\}\mathbb{P}\{\bar{X}_{\bar{t}^{\prime},\bar{\Omega}^{\prime}}\geq u_{n}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2b_{k}}\}.

(6.9)

In terms of the above notations, we have

\displaystyle\textnormal{Cov}[\mathds{1}_{\bar{t},\bar{\Omega}}(u_{n}),\mathds{1}_{\bar{t}^{\prime},\bar{\Omega}^{\prime}}(u_{n})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\{(\bar{X}_{\bar{t},\bar{\Omega}},\bar{X}_{\bar{t}^{\prime},\bar{\Omega}^{\prime}})\geq u_{n}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}\}-\mathbb{P}\{\bar{X}_{\bar{t},\bar{\Omega}}\geq u_{n}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2b_{k}}\}\mathbb{P}\{\bar{X}_{\bar{t},\bar{\Omega}}\geq u_{n}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2b_{k}}\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[\mathbb{P}\{(\bar{X}_{\bar{t},\bar{\Omega}},\bar{X}_{\bar{t}^{\prime},\bar{\Omega}^{\prime}})\geq u_{n}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}\}-\mathbb{P}\{\bar{Z}_{\hat{\rho}_{(\bar{t},\,\bar{\Omega}),(\bar{t}^{\prime},\bar{\Omega}^{\prime})}}\geq u_{n}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}\}]

\displaystyle+\;[\mathbb{P}\{\bar{Z}_{\hat{\rho}_{(\bar{t},\,\bar{\Omega}),(\bar{t}^{\prime},\bar{\Omega}^{\prime})}}\geq u_{n}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}\}-\mathbb{P}\{\bar{Z}_{0}\geq u_{n}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}\}].

Since the last two differences are positive on account of Lemma
2.5
, we have the desired result.

•

Arguments for Statement
2
: As
(\bar{t},\,\bar{\Omega})\neq(\bar{t}^{\prime},\bar{\Omega}^{\prime}),
there exists
\alpha^{\prime}\in\{-1,+1\}
and
\omega^{\prime}\in\bar{\Omega}^{\prime}
such that

\bar{t}^{\prime}+\alpha^{\prime}\bar{e}_{\omega^{\prime}}\neq\bar{t}+\alpha\bar{e}_{\omega}

(6.10)

for all
\alpha\in\{-1,+1\}
and
\omega\in\bar{\Omega}.
Hence, by dropping some variables, we have

\mathbb{P}\{(\bar{X}_{\bar{t},\bar{\Omega}},\bar{X}_{\bar{t}^{\prime},\bar{\Omega}^{\prime}})\geq u_{n}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}\}\leq\mathbb{P}\{(\bar{X}_{\bar{t},\bar{\Omega}},X_{\bar{t}^{\prime}+\alpha^{\prime}\bar{e}_{\omega^{\prime}}})\geq u_{n}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2b_{k}+1}\}.

Again, as (
6.10
) holds,
\Cr{a:r2LAsm}
and
\Cr{a:r1Asm}
imply that
\textnormal{Cov}[(X_{\bar{t}+\alpha\bar{e}_{\omega}},X_{\bar{t}^{\prime}+\alpha^{\prime}\bar{e}_{\omega^{\prime}}})]\leq\rho_{1}
for all
\alpha\in\{-1,+1\}
and
\omega\in\bar{\Omega}.
Using Lemma
2.5
, we then have

\mathbb{P}\{(\bar{X}_{\bar{t},\bar{\Omega}},X_{\bar{t}^{\prime}+\alpha^{\prime}\bar{e}_{\omega^{\prime}}})\geq u\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2b_{k}+1}\}\leq\mathbb{P}\{(\bar{X}_{\bar{0},\langle b_{k}\rangle},X_{\bar{0}})\geq u\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2b_{k}+1}\}.

The desired result now follows from Lemma
5.5
.
5
.

•

Arguments for Statement
3
: Since
q\geq 1
and
\Cr{a:r2LAsm}
holds, we have
\tilde{\rho}_{(\bar{t},\,\bar{\Omega}),(\bar{t}^{\prime},\bar{\Omega}^{\prime})}=\rho_{q}\in[0,\rho_{1}].
Hence, as discussed above, the random vector
\bar{Z}_{\rho_{q}}
is well defined.

From Lemma
2.5
, observe that

\textnormal{Cov}[\mathds{1}_{\bar{t},\bar{\Omega}}(u_{n}),\mathds{1}_{\bar{t}^{\prime},\bar{\Omega}^{\prime}}(u_{n})]\leq\mathbb{P}\{\bar{Z}_{\rho_{q}}\geq u_{n}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}\}.

With regards to the expression on the right, the notations from Lemma
2.4
have the following values:
i=4b_{k}
and
M=W_{2b_{k},2b_{k}}(\rho_{2},\rho_{q}).
From Lemma
A.2
, it is easy to see that
\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}
is an eigenvector of
M
with eigenvalue
1+(2b_{k}-1)\rho_{2}+2b_{k}\rho_{q}.
Hence,

\bar{\Delta}=u_{n}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}\;M^{-1}=\frac{u_{n}}{1+(2b_{k}-1)\rho_{2}+2b_{k}\rho_{q}}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}>0,

where the positivity holds due to
\Cr{a:r2LAsm}.
Having verified the Savage condition, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\{\bar{Z}_{\rho_{q}}\geq u_{n}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k})}\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{[1+(2b_{k}-1)\rho_{2}+2b_{k}\rho_{q}]^{4b_{k}}}{(2\pi)^{2b_{k}}(1-\rho_{2})^{2b_{k}-1}\sqrt{[1+(2b_{k}-1)\rho_{2}]^{2}-4b_{k}^{2}\rho_{q}^{2}}}u_{n}^{-4b_{k}}\exp\left[\frac{-2a_{k}u_{n}^{2}}{1+2a_{k}\rho_{q}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{[1+(2b_{k}-1)\rho_{2}+2b_{k}\rho_{1}]^{4b_{k}}}{(2\pi)^{2b_{k}}(1-\rho_{2})^{2b_{k}-1}\sqrt{[1+(2b_{k}-1)\rho_{2}]^{2}-4b_{k}^{2}\rho_{1}^{2}}}u_{n}^{-4b_{k}}\exp\left[\frac{-2a_{k}u_{n}^{2}}{1+2a_{k}\rho_{q}}\right],

where the first relation is due to Lemma
2.4
and the determinant formula in Lemma
A.2
, while the last relation holds since
\rho_{q}\leq\rho_{1}.
The desired result is now easy to see.

•

Arguments for Statement
4
: Observe that

\displaystyle\textnormal{Cov}[\mathds{1}_{\bar{t},\bar{\Omega}}(u_{n}),\mathds{1}_{\bar{t}^{\prime},\bar{\Omega}^{\prime}}(u_{n})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\{(\bar{X}_{\bar{t},\bar{\Omega}},\bar{X}_{\bar{t}^{\prime},\bar{\Omega}^{\prime}})\geq u_{n}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}\}-\mathbb{P}\{\bar{X}_{\bar{t},\bar{\Omega}}\geq u_{n}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2b_{k}}\}\mathbb{P}\{\bar{X}_{\bar{t}^{\prime},\bar{\Omega}^{\prime}}\geq u_{n}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2b_{k}}\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\{\bar{Z}_{\rho_{q}}\geq u_{n}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}\}-\mathbb{P}\{\bar{Z}_{0}\geq u_{n}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\rho_{q}}\frac{\partial}{\partial\mu}\left[\mathbb{P}\{\bar{Z}_{\mu}\geq u_{n}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}\}\right]\mathrm{d\mu}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\rho_{q}\;\max_{\mu\in(0,\rho_{q})}\left|\frac{\partial}{\partial\mu}\left[\mathbb{P}\{\bar{Z}_{\mu}\geq u_{n}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}\}\right]\right|,

where the second relation follows from Lemma
2.5
and (
6.9
) and the third relation follows from the second fundamental theorem of Calculus. The latter applies since
\rho_{q}\leq\rho_{1},
which holds due to
\Cr{a:r2LAsm},
and because
\mathbb{P}\{\bar{Z}_{\mu}\geq u_{n}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}\},
as a function of
\mu,
is continuous over
[0,\rho_{1}]
and differentiable in
(0,\rho_{1}),
which itself follows from Lemma
A.4
. This latter result also shows that, for
\mu\in(0,\rho_{q}),

\left|\frac{\partial}{\partial\mu}\left[\mathbb{P}\{\bar{Z}_{\mu}\geq u_{n}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}\}\right]\right|\leq O\left(u_{n}^{-(4b_{k}-2)}\exp\left[\frac{-2a_{k}u_{n}^{2}}{1+2a_{k}\rho_{q}}\right]\right).

The desired result is now easy to see.

This completes the proof.
∎

We now obtain the bounds discussed in Lemma
5.8
.

Proof of Lemma
5.8
.

As we have done so far, we talk about each statement separately.

•

Arguments for Statement
1
: Our approach here is a mixture of ideas from the proofs of Lemmas
5.5
.
3
and
5.7
.
4
.

For
1\leq j\leq b_{k},
let
W_{j,2b_{k}-j}(\rho_{2},\rho_{3})
and
Q_{2b_{k},j,j}(\rho_{2},\rho_{3},\mu)
be defined as in (
6.1
) and (
6.2
), respectively. For brevity, and since
\rho_{2},\rho_{3},
and
k
are constants, we shall denote these matrices as
W_{j}
and
Q_{j}(\mu),
respectively. These simplified notations apply only in this proof; the reader should not confuse
W_{j}
here with
W_{m}(\rho)
defined above (
6.1
).

We claim that there exist
\delta^{N}>0
such that, for all
\mu\in[0,\delta^{N}]
and
1\leq j\leq b_{k},
the following conditions hold:

1.

Q_{j}(\mu)
is positive definite, hence, invertible; further, its maximum eigenvalue is bounded from above by
\kappa\geq 0,
a constant which is independent of
\mu
and
j;

2.

for all large enough
u,

\mathbb{P}\{\bar{Y}_{\mu,j}\geq u\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}\}\leq\mathbb{P}\{\bar{Y}_{\mu,1}\geq u\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}\},
where
\bar{Y}_{\mu,j}\sim N(\bar{0},Q_{j}(\mu));
this random vector is well-defined since
Q_{j}(\mu)
is a symmetric positive define matrix.

Let
\bar{Z}_{\mu}=\bar{Y}_{\mu,1},

\mu\in[0,\delta^{N}].
Assuming the above claim to be true, we now prove the desired result.

We first prove (
5.23
). Using (
5.21
), note that

\mathbb{P}\{\bar{Z}_{\mu}\geq u_{n}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}\}-\left[\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}^{{}^{N}}_{\bar{0},k}(u_{n})]\right]^{2}=\mathbb{P}\{\bar{Z}_{\mu}\geq u_{n}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}\}-\mathbb{P}\{\bar{Z}_{0}\geq u_{n}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}\}.

The non-negativity is now a simple consequence of Lemma
2.5
. To establish the decay rate, set
m=2b_{k},

\rho=\rho_{2},
and
\mu^{\prime}=\rho_{3}.
Because
\Cr{a:r0Asm},\Cr{a:r2Asm}
and
\Cr{a:rqAsm}
hold, we have (
6.3
). Further, from condition
1
above, it follows that implications of Lemma
A.8
hold in relation to the matrix
Q_{j}(\mu)
for
\mu\in[0,\delta^{N}]
and
1\leq j\leq b_{k}.
Now, by arguing as in the proof of Lemma
5.7
.
4
and making use of Lemma
A.10
, it is easy to see that the desired result holds.

Let
(\bar{t},\,G),(\bar{t}^{\prime},\,G^{\prime})
be as in the statement with
|V_{1}|=|V^{\prime}_{1}|=j.
Also, let
V
and
V^{\prime}
be the vertex sets of
G
and
G^{\prime},
respectively. From Lemma
4.14
, recall that for each

(resp.

\bar{t}_{i_{2}}\in V\setminus V_{1}
(resp.

), we have
\|\bar{t}_{i_{1}}-\bar{t}_{i_{2}}\|_{1}\geq 3.
Further, whenever

(resp.

) or
\bar{t}_{i_{1}},\bar{t}_{i_{2}}\in V\setminus V_{1}

), we have from Corollary

that
\|\bar{t}_{i_{1}}-\bar{t}_{i_{2}}\|\geq 2.
Using (
5.18
) and the fact that
\Cr{a:r0Asm}
and
\Cr{a:rqAsm}
hold, after permuting the coordinates of
\bar{X}_{\bar{t},G}
and
\bar{X}_{\bar{t}^{\prime},G^{\prime}}
if necessary, it then follows from Lemma
2.5
that

\mathbb{P}\{(\bar{X}_{\bar{t},G},\bar{X}_{\bar{t}^{\prime},G^{\prime}})\geq u_{n}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}\}\leq\mathbb{P}\{\bar{Y}_{\mu,j}\geq u_{n}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}\}.

Now, by using condition
2
above, we get (
5.24
).

It remains to show the claim above. Towards this, we first prove the alternate claim: there exists
\delta^{N}>0
so that, for
\mu\in[0,\delta^{N}]
and
1\leq j\leq b_{k},
(a) condition
1
holds; (b) there exist suitable constants (with respect to
\mu
and
j
) which bound, from below and above, each of
\bar{\Delta}^{\prime}_{\mu,j}:=\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}Q_{j}^{-1}(\mu),

\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}[Q_{j}^{-1}(\mu)]\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}^{\top},
and
|Q_{j}(\mu)|;
and, finally, (c) if
j\geq 2,
then

\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}[Q_{j}^{-1}(\mu)]\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}^{\top}-\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}[Q_{1}^{-1}(\mu)]\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}^{\top}\geq C^{N}>0,

where
C^{N}
is some constant independent of
\mu
and
j.

Because
\Cr{a:r0Asm},

\Cr{a:r2Asm},
and
\Cr{a:rqAsm}
hold, recall that we have (
6.3
). Hence, it follows from Lemma
A.8
that there exists some
\delta\equiv\delta(2b_{k},\rho_{2},\rho_{3})>0
and
\kappa\equiv\kappa(2b_{k},\rho_{2},\rho_{3})>0
such that, for
\mu\in[0,\delta]
and
1\leq j\leq b_{k},
the matrix
Q_{j}(\mu)
is positive definite; further, its maximum eigenvalue is bounded from above by
\kappa.

Separately, observe that

\bar{\Delta}^{\prime}_{0,j}=\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}Q_{j}^{-1}(0)=(\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2b_{k}}W_{j}^{-1},\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2b_{k}}W_{j}^{-1})>0,

where the second relation follows from the definition of
Q_{j}(0),
while the last relation holds as in (
6.4
) which itself is true due to (
6.3
).

Also, note that, for
2\leq j\leq b_{k},

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}\,[Q^{-1}_{j}(0)]\,\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}^{\top}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\,[\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2b_{k}}\,W_{j}^{-1}\,\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2b_{k}}^{\top}]

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle 2\,[\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2b_{k}}\,W_{1}^{-1}\,\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{2b_{k}}^{\top}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}\,[Q^{-1}_{1}(0)]\,\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}^{\top}

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle 0,

where the first and the third relation follows from the definition of
Q_{j}(0),
the second one holds as in (
6.5
), while the last one is true as in (
6.4
); recall, both (
6.4
) and (
6.5
) are themselves true due to (
6.3
).

Further, for
1\leq j\leq b_{k},
we have

|Q_{j}(0)|=|W_{j}|^{2}>0,

where the first relation follows by definition, while the last one follows from Lemma
A.3
in which necessary conditions holds due to (
6.3
).

Lastly, since
\rho_{2}
and
\rho_{3}
are constants satisfying (
6.3
), we also have from their definitions that each of
\bar{\Delta}^{\prime}_{0,j},

\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}Q_{j}^{-1}(0)\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}^{\top},
and
|Q_{j}(0)|
is finite for all
j.

Using the above arguments and then invoking continuity of rational functions, it is now easy to see that there exists some positive number smaller than
\delta,
which we denote by
\delta^{N}
henceforth, such that our alternate claim holds. With this
\delta^{N},
we now establish condition
2
.

Let
u>0,

\mu\in[0,\delta^{N}],
and
j
be so that
2\leq j\leq b_{k}.
Also, let
\bar{\Delta}_{\mu,j}=u\bar{\Delta}^{\prime}_{\mu,j}.
Clearly,
\bar{\Delta}_{\mu,j}>0
for each
j.
Hence, it follows from Lemma
2.4
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\{\bar{Y}_{\mu,j}\geq u\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(2\pi)^{-2b_{k}}|Q_{j}(\mu)|^{-1/2}\exp\left[-u^{2}[\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}Q_{j}^{-1}(\mu)\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}^{\top}]/2\right]\left[\textstyle\prod_{l=1}^{4b_{k}}\bar{\Delta}_{\mu,j}(l)\right]^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(2\pi)^{-2b_{k}}u^{-4b_{k}}|Q_{j}(\mu)|^{-1/2}\exp\left[-u^{2}[\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}Q_{j}^{-1}(\mu)\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}^{\top}]/2\right]\left[\textstyle\prod_{l=1}^{4b_{k}}\bar{\Delta}^{\prime}_{\mu,j}(l)\right]^{-1}.

Similarly, by using the lower bound in Lemma
2.4
, we have

\mathbb{P}\{\bar{Y}_{\mu,1}\geq u\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}\}\geq\left[1-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{l_{1},l_{2}=1}^{4b_{k}}\frac{M_{l_{1}l_{2}}(\mu)[1+\mathds{1}_{l_{1}l_{2}}]}{u^{2}\bar{\Delta}^{\prime}_{\mu,1}(l_{1})\bar{\Delta}^{\prime}_{\mu,1}(l_{2})}\right]\\
(2\pi)^{-2b_{k}}u^{-4b_{k}}|Q_{1}(\mu)|^{-1/2}\exp\left[-u^{2}[\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}Q_{1}^{-1}(\mu)\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}^{\top}]/2\right]\left[\textstyle\prod_{l=1}^{4b_{k}}\bar{\Delta}^{\prime}_{\mu,1}(l)\right]^{-1},

where
M_{l_{1}l_{2}}(\mu)
is the
l_{1}l_{2}-
th entry of
Q_{1}^{-1}(\mu).
Since
\mathds{1}_{l_{1}l_{2}}\leq 1,
note that

1-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{l_{1},l_{2}=1}^{4b_{k}}\frac{M_{l_{1}l_{2}}(\mu)[1+\mathds{1}_{l_{1}l_{2}}]}{u^{2}\bar{\Delta}^{\prime}_{\mu,1}(l_{1})\bar{\Delta}^{\prime}_{\mu,1}(l_{2})}\geq 1-\frac{\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}Q_{1}^{-1}(\mu)\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}^{\top}}{u^{2}\bar{\Delta}^{\prime}_{\mu,1}(l_{1})\bar{\Delta}^{\prime}_{\mu,1}(l_{2})}.

Cancelling off the common terms and using part (c) of our alternate claim, it now follows that

\frac{\mathbb{P}\{\bar{Y}_{\mu,j}\geq u\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}\}}{\mathbb{P}\{\bar{Y}_{\mu,1}\geq u\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}\}}\leq\frac{h_{j}(\mu)\exp\left[-u^{2}C^{N}/2\right]}{\left[1-g(\mu)/u^{2}\right]h_{1}(\mu)}

for some suitably defined continuous functions
g,h_{j}:[0,\delta^{N}]\to\mathbb{R},
each of which is bounded from below and above by constants (with respect to
\mu
and
j
) in
(0,\infty).
Consequently, and because
C^{N}>0,
it follows that for all sufficiently large enough
u,

\frac{\mathbb{P}\{\bar{Y}_{\mu,j}\geq u\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}\}}{\mathbb{P}\{\bar{Y}_{\mu,1}\geq u\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}}\}}\leq 1.

This verifies Condition
2
, as desired.

•

Arguments for Statement
2
: Let
W_{1,2b_{k}}(\rho_{2},\rho_{1})
and
Q_{2b_{k}+1,1,1}(\rho_{2},\rho_{1},\mu)
be defined as in (
6.1
) and (
6.2
), respectively. Set
m=2b_{k}+1,

\rho=\rho_{2},
and
\mu^{\prime}=\rho_{1}.
Because
\Cr{a:r0Asm},\Cr{a:r1Asm},
and
\Cr{a:r2LAsm}
hold, we have
\rho_{2},\rho_{1}\in[0,1).
Since
\Cr{a:ComC}(k)
also holds, it follows from Lemma
A.9
that there exists some
\delta^{L}=\delta(2b_{k}+1,\rho_{2},\rho_{1})>0
and
\kappa\equiv\kappa(2b_{k}+1,\rho_{2},\rho_{1})>0
such that the matrix
Q_{2b_{k}+1,1,1}(\rho_{2},\rho_{1},\mu)
is positive definite and, hence, invertible; further, its maximum eigenvalue is bounded from above by
\kappa.
Now, since
Q_{2b_{k}+1,1,1}(\rho_{2},\rho_{1},\mu)
is also symmetric, it follows that the random variable
\bar{Z}_{\mu}\sim N(\bar{0},Q_{2b_{k}+1,1,1}(\rho_{2},\rho_{3},\mu))
is well defined for all
\mu\in[0,\delta^{L}].

Using (
5.22
), observe that

\mathbb{P}\{\bar{Z}_{\mu}\geq u_{n}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}+2}\}-\left[\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}^{{}^{L}}_{\bar{0},k}(u_{n})]\right]^{2}=\mathbb{P}\{\bar{Z}_{\mu}\geq u_{n}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}+2}\}-\mathbb{P}\{\bar{Z}_{0}\geq u_{n}\bar{\mathbf{1}}_{4b_{k}+2}\}.

Now, using Lemma
A.10
and arguing as in the proof of Lemma
5.7
.
4
, we get that (
5.25
) holds.

We now prove (
5.26
). Let
k\geq 1.
Consider
(\bar{t},\,G),(\bar{t}^{\prime},\,G^{\prime})
as in the statement. As argued in the proof of Lemma
5.5
.
6
, it is easy to see from (

) and (

) that
|V|\geq 2k+3
(resp.
|V^{\prime}|\geq 2k+3
); further, there exist
2k+2
vertices in
V
(resp.
V^{\prime}
) such that their pairwise
\|\cdot\|_{1}-
distance is at least

Retaining only these
2k+2
vertices and an additional vertex from remaining ones both in

and

and then permuting them if necessary, it is not difficult to see using the definition of
\tilde{\rho}^{L}_{(\bar{t},\,G),(\bar{t}^{\prime},\,G^{\prime})}
(given below (

)) and Lemma

that (

) holds.

Using arguments similar to those above and the ones used in the proof of the

case of Lemma
5.5
.

, it is easy to see that the desired result holds for the
